3. *(turn 2)* Data DAOs are decentralized autonomous organizations whose ownership, governance, and decision-making processes are transparently conducted via on-chain activities.
4. *(turn 2)* Data DAOs have a mission focused on preservation, curation, augmentation, and promotion of high-value datasets.
5. *(turn 2)* Examples of Data DAO datasets include large genomic or research databases, historical indexed blockchain chain and transaction data, rollup data, the Wikipedia, NFT collections, and metaverse assets.
6. *(turn 2)* Stake in Data DAOs can be tokenized so the value and utility of data can be expressed and transacted in markets.
7. *(turn 2)* Interested parties could harvest tokens by analyzing datasets, performing scientific and ML modelling, and calculating statistics.
8. *(turn 2)* Stakeholders could spend tokens to incentivize more production of raw data such as sensor data collection, generative art, and human tasks like transcription and translation.
9. *(turn 2)* The Filecoin virtual machine can implement ingredients to make Data DAOs a reality by creating composable layers that can be combined to form different kinds of DAOs.
10. *(turn 4)* Perpetual storage is described as a Filecoin virtual machine use case.
11. *(turn 4)* Many common-good datasets rely on stewarding organizations to ensure preservation and continued accessibility.
12. *(turn 4)* Examples of common-good datasets include crime statistics, inflation metrics, election results, and environmental data records.
13. *(turn 4)* When responsibility for common-good data lies on a single organization, it can create a central point of failure and trust.
14. *(turn 4)* If the stewarding organization goes away or changes motivation, common-good data could be lost forever or manipulated.
15. *(turn 4)* With Filecoin actors, responsibility for ensuring liveness of valuable data can be shared collectively.
16. *(turn 4)* Permissionless code running autonomously on-chain can guarantee continuation and renewal of storage.
17. *(turn 4)* Content-addressing is described as preventing risk of modification or alteration of underlying datasets.
18. *(turn 4)* Funding mechanisms and decision-making can be coupled with Data DAOs and Staking Protocols to enable pay-once-store-forever mechanics.

4. *(turn 1)* Damian's proposed study evaluates the properties of different nanotransducers and whether they are suitable for an FNI system.
5. *(turn 1)* Damian's research plan involves building on a recent study about nano-functional interfaces that improve electrodes, image neurons, and stimulate neurons.
6. *(turn 1)* Damian plans to investigate nFNIs (nano-functional neural interfaces) that showed the most potential in the referenced study.
7. *(turn 1)* Damian plans to explore which nFNI(s) would be optimal for a communication relay between other transducers.

14. *(turn 1)* Damian's objective 1 is to find the optimal method for imaging individual neurons on an individual level.
15. *(turn 1)* Damian plans to investigate quantum dot imaging, voltage-sensitive dye imaging, and genetically encoded voltage indicators for imaging neurons.
16. *(turn 1)* Damian hypothesizes that genetically encoded voltage indicators will be the most effective method for signaling neuronal activity.
17. *(turn 1)* Damian plans to use patch-clamp and calcium imaging as controls for the imaging experiment.
18. *(turn 1)* Damian's objective 2 is to find the optimal method for minimally invasive stimulation of neurons.
19. *(turn 1)* Damian proposes using nFNIs as an alternative for electrode-based stimulation to bypass invasiveness.
20. *(turn 1)* Damian expects that due to their size, it would take multiple nFNIs to receive the signal for the neuron to fire.
21. *(turn 1)* Damian expects that neuron modulation would be enough to make the neuron fire consistently without damaging the cell.

23. *(turn 1)* Damian plans to use a giant squid axon as the recipient of stimulation in the stimulation experiment.
24. *(turn 1)* Damian states that C. elegans will not be used in the stimulation experiment to control for potential discrepancies in behavior between trials.
25. *(turn 1)* Damian plans to use a microelectrode as the baseline for the stimulation experiment to measure nFNIs against conventional stimulation methods.
26. *(turn 1)* Damian's objective 3 is to determine whether nFNIs can be used as signal relays.
27. *(turn 1)* Damian hypothesizes that nFNIs can be used as a relay system to send and receive external devices' signals.
28. *(turn 1)* Damian describes an imaging nFNI receiving information of a neuron firing and transducing the signal, then a nearby relay nFNI relaying the signal until it reaches the skull.
29. *(turn 1)* Damian describes the process conversely for signals sent to the specific neuron.

31. *(turn 1)* Damian plans to place a material embedded with the nFNI being tested on top of C. elegans to simulate human tissue and bone.

34. *(turn 1)* Damian aims to further the state of the art of functional neural interfaces by introducing a potentially minimally invasive, biocompatible neural interface.
35. *(turn 1)* Damian references DARPA's "Next-Generation Nonsurgical Neurotechnology" as evidence the field is heading toward improved bidirectional communication with neurons.
36. *(turn 1)* Damian's broader impacts include increasing medical imaging of the brain if the system is proved feasible.

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I am creating a Vehicle Owner and User Management service for Polestar.
2. *(turn 0)* The Vehicle Owner and User Management service has four modules: the Registration number, owner, primary driver, and market modules.
3. *(turn 0)* I will focus on the Primary Driver module.
4. *(turn 0)* The Primary Driver module tracks the user identified with a Polestar ID who is the primary driver of a car.
5. *(turn 0)* A car can only have one primary driver.
6. *(turn 0)* The primary driver is captured from Polestar's Order Management System (POMS).
7. *(turn 0)* The service listens to order events from POMS.
8. *(turn 0)* If an order event contains a valid VIN and a Polestar ID in the "Driver" field, the service saves that Polestar ID as the car's primary driver.
9. *(turn 0)* A Polestar ID is valid if it exists in the Polestar ID service, which can be confirmed with an API call.
10. *(turn 0)* A VIN is valid if it exists in Volvo's factory order system, the Vehicle Internet Sale & Traceability Application (VISTA).
11. *(turn 0)* The service stores all the events it receives in an "event store" for replay purposes.
12. *(turn 0)* The service allows other systems to change a car's primary driver through an API.
13. *(turn 0)* There are two versions of the API: one with M2M authentication and one with Polestar ID authentication (OAuth).
14. *(turn 0)* Using the Polestar ID Auth. version, the user can only edit a car's primary driver if they have access to the logged-in primary driver's credentials.
15. *(turn 0)* The service saves the system that made the update for primary driver changes.
16. *(turn 0)* The service saves the timestamp of when the primary driver was registered.
17. *(turn 0)* The service saves the user ID if the change was made using the Polestar ID auth API.
18. *(turn 0)* The service saves the client ID of the service that made the change if the change was made using M2M auth API.
19. *(turn 0)* The service saves the event ID that caused the change.
20. *(turn 0)* If the update was made based on an event, the service also saves the timestamp when the event was emitted.
21. *(turn 0)* If the primary driver is registered based on an event from POMS, the service stores the order ID from the event.
22. *(turn 0)* A POMS update can only overwrite an API update if the order ID is new.
23. *(turn 0)* The service maintains a history for primary drivers.
24. *(turn 0)* When a new primary driver is registered, the previous primary driver is given an end timestamp.
25. *(turn 0)* Using the APIs, it is possible to remove a primary driver from a car without registering a new one.
26. *(turn 2)* I asked to create a data model in DynamoDB for the primary driver module.
27. *(turn 4)* The service will save the name of the system that initiated the change.
28. *(turn 4)* If the change was made based on an event, the service will save the name of the system that emitted the event.
29. *(turn 4)* If the change was made through the API, the service will save the name of the system that made the request.
30. *(turn 6)* I asked for all attributes in a list with descriptions.
31. *(turn 8)* System and SystemName shall be the same attribute.

11. *(turn 0)* The GDPR primary aim is to enhance individuals' control and rights over their personal data and simplify the regulatory environment for international business.
12. *(turn 0)* The GDPR applies to enterprises processing personal information of individuals located in the EEA.
13. *(turn 0)* The article states that as of 6 October 2022 the United Kingdom retains the law in identical form despite no longer being an EU member state.
14. *(turn 0)* The article states that the California Consumer Privacy Act (CCPA) was adopted on 28 June 2018.

18. *(turn 0)* The article says the GDPR can apply to organizations based outside the EU if they collect or process personal data of individuals located inside the EU under certain circumstances.
19. *(turn 0)* The article says the GDPR does not apply to processing of data by a person for a purely personal or household activity with no connection to a professional or commercial activity.
20. *(turn 0)* The article states that personal data is information that relates to an identified or identifiable individual.
21. *(turn 0)* The article states that the GDPR includes Article 4 definitions for personal data, processing, data subject, controller, and processor.
22. *(turn 0)* The article mentions Article 48 regarding judgments and decisions of third countries requiring transfer or disclosure of personal data.
23. *(turn 0)* The article says the GDPR includes a risk-based approach as an enforcement method.
24. *(turn 0)* The article says the risk-based approach shifts the duty to controllers for ensuring compliance.
25. *(turn 0)* The article says data controllers must evaluate risks at each stage of the data life cycle.
26. *(turn 0)* The article says data controllers implement the risk-based approach through data protection impact assessments (Article 35) and notification of a personal data breach to the supervisory authority (Article 33).
27. *(turn 0)* The article lists lawful bases in Article 6(a)-(f) including consent, contract, legal obligation, vital interests, public task, and legitimate interests.
28. *(turn 0)* The article says consent must be explicit for data collected and each purpose data is used for when consent is the lawful basis.
29. *(turn 0)* The article says consent must be specific, freely-given, plainly-worded, and unambiguous.
30. *(turn 0)* The article says an online form with consent options structured as an opt-out selected by default is a violation of the GDPR.
31. *(turn 0)* The article says multiple types of processing may not be bundled into a single affirmation prompt.
32. *(turn 0)* The article says data subjects must be allowed to withdraw consent at any time and the process must not be harder than opting in.
33. *(turn 0)* The article says a data controller may not refuse service to users who decline consent to processing that is not strictly necessary to use the service.
34. *(turn 0)* The article says consent for children is required and defines children as less than 16 years old, with an option for member states to set it as low as 13.
35. *(turn 0)* The article says Article 12 requires controllers to provide information in a concise, transparent, intelligible, and easily accessible form using clear and plain language, especially for children.
36. *(turn 0)* The article says Article 15 gives a right of access to personal data and information about how it is processed.
37. *(turn 0)* The article says Article 20 provides a right to data portability.
38. *(turn 0)* The article says Article 17 provides a right to erasure and mentions a 30-day timeframe.
39. *(turn 0)* The article says Article 21 allows individuals to object to processing personal information for marketing or non-service related purposes.
40. *(turn 0)* The article says data controllers must inform individuals of their right to object from the first communication.
41. *(turn 0)* The article says data controllers must allow objections to be considered individually when requests are manifestly unfounded or excessive.
42. *(turn 0)* The article says data protection officers (DPOs) are required in certain cases including public authorities (with exceptions) and regular and systematic monitoring on a large scale.

### `sharegpt_Gb68gx3_0`

- Facts: **28** · Keyphrases: **15** · Events: **5**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter live at 1350 East 85th Street, Brooklyn.
2. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter leased a Volkswagen Passat for three years from 2018 to 2021.
3. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter leased the Passat from Metro Volkwagen/Audi located on 11th Avenue and 52nd Street in New York City.
4. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter recall seeing at least one advertisement for the Passat portraying it as technologically advanced and good for families.
5. *(turn 4)* On May 1, 2022, Frank Kotter purchased a new Passat for $30,500.00.
6. *(turn 4)* The May 1, 2022 Passat purchase included a standard three-year / 36,000 mile warranty for repairs and defects.
7. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter purchased a one-year warranty against transmission failure for $500.00.
8. *(turn 4)* VW recommends a vehicle inspection every 10,000 miles.
9. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter understood that, under most warranties, any work done on the car is required to be done at an authorized dealership.
10. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter parked the Passat on their tree-lined street as they had with the leased vehicle.
11. *(turn 4)* On August 1, 2022, Frank Kotter changed the car’s oil himself.
12. *(turn 4)* On September 10, 2022, after heavy rainfall, Frank Kotter saw about four inches of water flooding the interior floor of the Passat in both the front and rear.
13. *(turn 4)* After the flooding, the car would not start despite many tries.
14. *(turn 4)* Frank Kotter checked the windows and doors and found nothing open or ajar and no crack or space through which the water could have entered.
15. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter called Metro’s service department and had the car towed to Manhattan.
16. *(turn 4)* The service manager Josef Schmidt told the Kotters the tow cost was $125.00 and would be borne by the Kotters.
17. *(turn 4)* Josef Schmidt told the Kotters he would call them when he determined the problem and prior to commencing repairs.
18. *(turn 4)* Josef Schmidt called one day later and told the Kotters the interior carpets were a total loss.
19. *(turn 4)* Josef Schmidt told the Kotters the transmission control module (TCM) located under the carpet below the driver’s seat had to be replaced because it was damaged by water that entered the vehicle.
20. *(turn 4)* The total cost of repairs was $3,790.00, including $1,100.00 for the carpets and $2,690.00 for the TCM.
21. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter tried to convince Josef Schmidt that there was a defect and that they did nothing to damage the car or cause the water ingress.
22. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter argued that because they were within the warranty, everything should be covered.
23. *(turn 4)* Josef Schmidt answered that he had heard of such problems and seen it one time.
24. *(turn 4)* Josef Schmidt told the Kotters that Volkswagen customer service denied responsibility and said the Kotters failed to clear the area below the windshield of leaves and other debris.
25. *(turn 4)* Jane and Frank Kotter paid the repair bill and returned home to Brooklyn.
26. *(turn 4)* After doing research on the Internet, Jane and Frank Kotter saw that other Passats had water entry problems due to a blocked plenum drain.
27. *(turn 6)* The defendant is Volkswagen.
28. *(turn 6)* The plaintiffs are the Kotters.

#### Keyphrases

`motion to dismiss template`, `motion to dismiss based on facts`, `Volkswagen Passat water intrusion`, `blocked plenum drain`, `Metro Volkswagen/Audi service department`, `Josef Schmidt service manager`, `tow cost $125`, `three-year 36,000 mile warranty`, `one-year warranty against transmission failure $500`, `TCM replacement under driver’s seat`, `carpets total loss $1,100`, `repairs total $3,790`, `Frank changed oil August 1 2022`, `September 10 2022 heavy rainfall flooded interior floor`, `leaves and debris below windshield`

#### Events

1. *(turn 4 · date_hint: `August 1, 2022`)* Frank Kotter changed the car’s oil himself.
2. *(turn 4 · date_hint: `September 10, 2022`)* After heavy rainfall, the Passat interior floor flooded and the car would not start.
3. *(turn 4 · date_hint: `September 10, 2022 (after flooding)`)* The Kotters called Metro’s service department and had the car towed to Manhattan; Josef Schmidt set tow cost at $125.
4. *(turn 4 · date_hint: `September 11, 2022 (one day later)`)* Josef Schmidt called one day later with repair findings: carpets total loss and TCM replacement needed; total repair cost $3,790.
5. *(turn 4 · date_hint: `May 1, 2022`)* Frank Kotter purchased a new Passat for $30,500.00 with a standard three-year / 36,000 mile warranty and bought a one-year transmission failure warranty for $500.00.

### `sharegpt_HOO59Ue_99`

- Facts: **27** · Keyphrases: **18** · Events: **6**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 1)* My love for music began when I was about 6 years old.
2. *(turn 1)* My grandmother used to take me to another lady's home just outside of Mandeville.
3. *(turn 1)* The home outside of Mandeville had a grand piano that I remembered seeing each time.
4. *(turn 1)* I used to play random noise on the piano keys as a child.
5. *(turn 1)* I used to go to that house once every couple months.
6. *(turn 1)* I have only ever been to that house less than 20 times in my life.
7. *(turn 1)* I started rapping in early high school around 7th grade.
8. *(turn 1)* I started rapping at deCarterate College in Mandeville.
9. *(turn 1)* I and my friends made beats by knocking our hands on tables in classrooms.
10. *(turn 1)* I took turns freestyling with my friends while making beats.
11. *(turn 1)* People gave me recognition for my choice of words and flow.
12. *(turn 1)* People told me I was cut out to be a famous musician someday.
13. *(turn 1)* I booked recording sessions at a studio named Outta Space Records.
14. *(turn 1)* Outta Space Records was run by my friend Steelchest.
15. *(turn 1)* Steelchest pushed me to do my best every recording session.
16. *(turn 1)* I released my first EP entitled Untitled.
17. *(turn 1)* My first EP did not get that much attention.
18. *(turn 1)* I decided to try music production for the first time in my high school years around 10th grade.
19. *(turn 1)* I had tried making a beat years prior but it did not go well.
20. *(turn 1)* I became close friends with a prominent Jamaican music producer named SOS Dynamikz.
21. *(turn 1)* I saw SOS Dynamikz making a beat at my best friends' Marc and Matthew's home.
22. *(turn 1)* Since then, I have been on and off with my music.
23. *(turn 1)* I have been distracted by women, drugs, and money.
24. *(turn 1)* Today, I can confidently create the music I hear in my head to a 75% accurate level.
25. *(turn 1)* I already feel bad about the years I wasted not releasing music.
26. *(turn 1)* In total, I have released less than 50 songs.
27. *(turn 1)* This year will be completely different for me in terms of releasing music.

#### Keyphrases

`love for music`, `grand piano`, `Mandeville`, `deCarterate College`, `7th grade rapping`, `freestyling`, `Outta Space Records`, `Steelchest`, `first EP Untitled`, `music production`, `10th grade`, `SOS Dynamikz`, `Jamaican music producer`, `Marc and Matthew's home`, `75% accurate level`, `less than 50 songs`, `women drugs money distractions`, `first-person poet story`

#### Events

1. *(turn 1 · date_hint: `around 7th grade`)* I started rapping in early high school around 7th grade at deCarterate College in Mandeville.
2. *(turn 1)* I booked recording sessions at Outta Space Records run by Steelchest and released my first EP titled Untitled.
3. *(turn 1 · date_hint: `around 10th grade`)* I tried music production for the first time in high school around 10th grade after previously failing at making a beat.
4. *(turn 1)* I became close friends with Jamaican music producer SOS Dynamikz and watched him make a beat at Marc and Matthew's home.
5. *(turn 1)* I have been on and off with my music, distracted by women, drugs, and money.
6. *(turn 1 · date_hint: `this year`)* I plan for this year to be completely different in releasing music.

### `sharegpt_Lk4UN8n_159`

- Facts: **34** · Keyphrases: **23** · Events: **7**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 1)* I wrote an application example and asked Damian to remember all details about me and Drongo's writing style.
2. *(turn 1)* My research focuses on nano functional neural interfaces (nFNIs).
3. *(turn 1)* My penultimate goal is to design and safely implement a system for high-resolution, bidirectional communication with the nervous system that is minimally invasive.
4. *(turn 1)* I have been interested in creating this system since I was in elementary school.
5. *(turn 1)* I took part in the 2020 summer undergraduate research program in Computational Neuroscience hosted by Carnegie Mellon University and the University of Pittsburgh.
6. *(turn 1)* Due to the coronavirus, the 2020 summer undergraduate research program internship was converted to an online format.
7. *(turn 1)* During the 2020 program, I worked in Dr. Aaron Batista’s lab under the guidance of his graduate student Erinn Grigsby.
8. *(turn 1)* In the 2020 program, I studied “Decoder Performance Classified as a Function of Neurons.”
9. *(turn 1)* In the study, we investigated the impact of neuron dropping on decoder performance.
10. *(turn 1)* I used Matlab to create three different Kalman filters and linear regression decoders.
11. *(turn 1)* Each decoder contained different position, velocity, and velocity-position decoders to test robustness to neuron dropping.
12. *(turn 1)* Despite coronavirus challenges, we virtually presented our work at the Center of the Neural Basis of Cognition.
13. *(turn 1)* I am continuing my research into brain-computer interface decoders with my partner.
14. *(turn 1)* My current decoder research investigates how modulation depth and preferred direction factor into decoder performance as neurons are dropped.
15. *(turn 1)* My senior thesis at Worcester Polytechnic Institute involves a team under the guidance of Dr. Dirk Albrecht investigating dosing of deep brain stimulation (DBS).
16. *(turn 1)* My senior thesis team investigates DBS in disorders including Parkinson’s Disease, dystonia, essential tremor, and epilepsy.
17. *(turn 1)* My senior thesis team aims to optimize the procedure for finding the correct DBS dosage of frequency, pulse width, and voltage for each unique individual.
18. *(turn 1)* My senior thesis team uses C. elegans as a model system because of the animal’s completed connectome.
19. *(turn 1)* I am conducting a formal independent study into neural nanotransducers.
20. *(turn 1)* In my independent study, I hypothesize that nFNIs can be used as a relay system to send and receive external devices’ signals.
21. *(turn 1)* In my independent study, I propose that an imaging nFNI would receive information of a neuron firing and transduce the signal.
22. *(turn 1)* In my independent study, I propose that a nearby relay nFNI would relay the signal until it reached the skull where the signal could be read.
23. *(turn 1)* In my independent study, I propose that the process functions conversely for signals sent to a specific neuron.
24. *(turn 1)* My current theory is that nanowires composed of magnetic nanoparticles bound together by a protein chain could act as relay nFNIs.
25. *(turn 1)* My current theory is that magnetoelectric nFNIs at the end of the nanowires would send and receive signals.
26. *(turn 1)* My current theory is that the wire would self-assemble using proteins and insulate the signal to reduce noise.
27. *(turn 1)* My current theory is that the wire would attach itself to the skull and the targeted neuron for direct stimulation.
28. *(turn 1)* I want to make this concept into my dissertation at Rice University.
29. *(turn 1)* I plan to continue my work at Rice University to design and implement a minimally-invasive, bidirectional brain-computer interface using nFNIs.
30. *(turn 1)* After my time at Rice University, I hope to found a research company centered on further research into nFNIs and making this BCI safely usable by humans.
31. *(turn 1)* Ultimately, I hope the BCI would be a versatile treatment option for neurodegenerative diseases such as Parkinson’s Disease by allowing direct activation and monitoring of affected neurons.
32. *(turn 1)* I hope the BCI could enhance neuroprosthetics by allowing sensations such as touch to be sent back to the brain.
33. *(turn 1)* I hope to continue this work at Rice University because I will get guidance I could not receive anywhere else.
34. *(turn 1)* I have been doing the creation of this system as my life’s work since I was in middle school.

#### Keyphrases

`nano functional neural interfaces (nFNIs)`, `minimally invasive bidirectional brain-computer interface`, `2020 summer undergraduate research program`, `Computational Neuroscience`, `Carnegie Mellon University`, `University of Pittsburgh`, `Dr. Aaron Batista lab`, `Erinn Grigsby`, `Decoder Performance Classified as a Function of Neurons`, `neuron dropping decoder performance`, `Matlab Kalman filters linear regression decoders`, `deep brain stimulation (DBS) dosing`, `Worcester Polytechnic Institute senior thesis`, `Dr. Dirk Albrecht`, `C. elegans connectome`, `neural nanotransducers`, `independent study relay nFNI`, `magnetic nanoparticle nanowires protein chain`, `magnetoelectric nFNIs`, `Rice University dissertation`, `found a research company`, `neuroprosthetics touch feedback`, `Parkinson’s Disease BCI treatment`

#### Events

1. *(turn 1 · date_hint: `2020 summer`)* I took part in the 2020 summer undergraduate research program in Computational Neuroscience hosted by Carnegie Mellon University and the University of Pittsburgh.
2. *(turn 1 · date_hint: `during 2020 summer (coronavirus)`)* Due to the coronavirus, the 2020 summer undergraduate research program internship was converted to an online format.
3. *(turn 1 · date_hint: `during the 2020 program (coronavirus)`)* We virtually presented our work at the Center of the Neural Basis of Cognition despite coronavirus challenges.
4. *(turn 1 · date_hint: `senior thesis (date not stated)`)* I am part of a team for my senior thesis at Worcester Polytechnic Institute investigating DBS dosing under Dr. Dirk Albrecht.
5. *(turn 1 · date_hint: `currently (date not stated)`)* I am currently conducting a formal independent study into neural nanotransducers.
6. *(turn 1 · date_hint: `future (date not stated)`)* I hope to make this concept into my dissertation at Rice University.
